To determine the shortest path between the two black nodes (F and I) in the provided graph, follow these steps:

1. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes of the black nodes:**
   - Node F: Connected to B, T.
   - Node I: Connected to H, Q, P.

2. **Identify the neighboring nodes of these immediate neighbors:**
   - Neighbors of B: E, S, T.
   - Neighbors of T: B, F.
   - Neighbors of H: G.
   - Neighbors of Q: R, G, O, J.
   - Neighbors of P: Q.

3. **Determine potential paths:**
   - From F to B: [F, B]
   - From F to T: [F, T]

   - From B to T: [B, T]
   - From B to E: [B, E]
   - From B to S: [B, S]

   - From T to B: [T, B]
   - From T to F: [T, F]

   - From H to G: [H, G]

   - From Q to R: [Q, R]
   - From Q to G: [Q, G]
   - From Q to O: [Q, O]
   - From Q to J: [Q, J]

   - From P to Q: [P, Q]

4. **Expand the search to find a path between F and I:**
   - Path via B:
     - B is connected to T, which does not lead to I.
     - B is connected to E, which does not lead to I.
     - B is connected to S, which does not lead to I.
   - Path via T:
     - T is connected to B, which does not lead to I.
     - T is connected to F.
   - Path via Q:
     - Q is connected to R, which does not lead to I.
     - Q is connected to G, which does not lead to I.
     - Q is connected to O, which does not lead to I.
     - Q is connected to J, which does not lead to I.
   - Path via H:
     - H is connected to G, which does not lead to I.
   - Path via P:
     - P is connected to Q.
     - Q is connected to G which goes via H and reaches I.

5. **Combine paths to find the shortest one:**
   - The shortest path from F to I:
     - F -> B -> H -> I - 3
     - F -> B -> T -> G -> Q -> P - 5

     The first path [F -> B -> H -> I] is the shortest path.

**Answer:**
